# Fact-check log — Medicare Advantage: Is It a Better Deal Than Original Medicare?

_Independent verification pass, run 2026-10-21 (web-sourced, not trusting the draft). Each claim gets a
verdict: **CONFIRMED**, **CORRECTED**, **UNRESOLVED** or **APPROXIMATE**. This is a REQUIRED gate, set
out under "Verification gate" in the episodes README. The show's differentiator is that this file is
real._

- **CONFIRMED** — the source says what the episode says.
- **CORRECTED** — the source says otherwise; the script was changed, and the change is listed under
  "Corrections applied to source" below. These stay in the file: they are the point of it.
- **UNRESOLVED** — could not be established from a source worth trusting, so it is not used on air.
- **APPROXIMATE** — checked, roughly right, and **deliberately not cited on air**. Not the same as
  UNRESOLVED: the claim was established, the *figure* just isn't precise enough to state. Use it when
  the direction is sound but a number would imply more precision than the source supports.

⛔ These four are the whole vocabulary. The site builder reads them to count the claims the public
site prints, and since package `HFIX` a verdict it does not recognise **fails the build** instead of
being skipped silently. A fifth verdict means changing both this file and the builder.

**Verification status:** adversarial-pass

---

**How this was checked.** A peg hunt first (2026-10-21): a blind sweep with every topic word banned
(Medicare, Medicare Advantage, Medigap, open enrollment, star ratings, prior authorization, networks and
the rest), the Federal Register's presidential documents, rules and notices for 2026-09-23 to 10-21,
the insurers and the federal watchdogs queried by name, then targeted reads of what it surfaced (its
record is the last section of this file). Then the deep-research-v3 engine (run wf_36749d26-a65: 41
agents, 39 of them on Sonnet, 3,578,643 subagent tokens; 19 findings; about 10 claims verified with the
primary opened; 106 claims not adjudicated; 1 flagged). Then a hand pass on the primary record, split
six ways by domain: **A, what it costs you** (the out-of-pocket comparison studies, the yearly cap,
premiums, drug coverage, the extras); **B, access and quality** (prior authorization and denials, the
Original Medicare comparator, networks and directories, health outcomes, star ratings); **B2, the
insurers' side and network rules** (the industry's own statements, the 2025 prior-authorization pledge,
network-adequacy law, cancer centers and dialysis, the star-rating recalculation); **C, switching and
exits** (the federal Medigap rights, state rules, who leaves Medicare Advantage, the 2027 exits,
enrollment and the subgroups the question names); **D, the taxpayer side** (MedPAC's payment estimate,
rebates, the Part B premium effect, the 2027 rates, billing audits, the referees and the industry's
rebuttals); and **D2, its continuation** (UnitedHealth and the Justice Department, GAO on coding, the
Health Affairs Forefront fight over MedPAC's estimate, the audit results and the Fifth Circuit appeal).
Together they checked **448 claims at the primary source**. Every primary relied on was downloaded and
saved with its text extracted (PDFs with pypdf, web pages as text), and is kept in the show's evidence
archive (not part of the published bundle). Several publishers served bot-challenge pages that were
**not** bypassed: Health Affairs Scholar and the Annals were read in their PubMed Central copy and
PubMed abstract instead; the critics' Health Affairs Forefront article was never read and is described
only through its opponents' rebuttal; two journal articles are known only through a media release and a
trade write-up, and those rows say so. GAO's site refused automated fetching, so its reports were read
through a text-rendering service; CBO's report and one UnitedHealth release were read from the Wayback
Machine's capture of the agency's own file. The main session then checked every relied-on quote against
the saved copies: **67 of 67** for hand pass A, **111 of 111** for B, **55 of 55** for B2, **81 of 81**
for C, **88 of 88** for D and **44 of 44** for D2. After the host's pitch rulings (2026-10-21), four
top-up hand passes added **84 rows** (Part 17): **E** the Medigap window at 65 and the 2027 costs, **F**
the cap's scope and the switch windows, **G1** the help programs for low-income people, **G2** free help
and broker pay; the main session re-checked their quotes with **0 misses**. The hand passes overrule the engine wherever they
differ. A claim only the engine made, and no hand pass checked, is kept **off air**. There was no scout
brief; the question's scoping note was used as a list of claims to test, never as a source.

**Counting rule:** one row per claim a hand pass checked, in the hand pass's own words, renumbered into
the parts below; the last words of each verdict name the pass and its row. Where two passes checked the
same figure (the quality-bonus totals, the rebates spent on drug coverage) both rows are kept, and they
agree. **Totals: 532 rows — CONFIRMED 434 · CORRECTED 36 · APPROXIMATE 42 · UNRESOLVED 20** (the
first 448 rows: 363 · 35 · 33 · 17; the 84 top-up rows: 71 · 1 · 9 · 3). (The
corrections table below has 78 rows: some corrections fix the wording or scope of a claim whose row is
CONFIRMED, and the peg hunt's errors are listed there even where the row records the current fact.)

**Reading the verdicts here.** "Engine" means the research engine's report: where a row quotes it, the
row records what reached the hand pass, and the verdict says what the source shows. A CORRECTED row
states the claim as it reached us, or the fix itself; the corrections table gives both. "Secondary" in a
source means the claim was checked against reporting because the primary could not be reached.
**[advocacy]**, **[company]** and **[party]** mark a source with a stake in the answer: the insurers'
trade groups and allied coalitions, an insurer or its consultant speaking for itself, a side in a live
dispute. APPROXIMATE rows are used, if at all, only as a direction or an attributed report, never as a
precise figure. UNRESOLVED rows are in the brief's "Off air" list. "Our arithmetic" marks the show's
own computation on quoted figures. Abbreviations: MA (Medicare Advantage), TM or FFS (traditional, or
Original, Medicare), MA-PD (an Advantage plan with drug coverage), PDP (a stand-alone drug plan), SNP
(a special needs plan; D-SNP for people with both Medicare and Medicaid), MAO (an Advantage insurer),
PA (prior authorization), SNF (skilled nursing facility), LTCH (long-term care hospital), IRF (inpatient
rehabilitation facility), MOOP (the yearly out-of-pocket limit), PMPM (per member per month), RADV
(Medicare's billing-audit program), SEP (special enrollment period), AEP (the annual open enrollment).
Every court, rulemaking and enforcement status is as of 2026-10-21.

---

## Part 17 — Top-ups after the pitch: the Medigap window at 65, the 2027 costs, the caps and windows, the help programs, and free help

_Four top-up hand passes run 2026-10-21 after the host's pitch rulings: **E** the federal Medigap open
enrollment period at 65 and the 2027 cost stack; **F** what the out-of-pocket cap covers, the January
switch window, Part B givebacks, a doctor leaving mid-year, hospice; **G1** the help programs for people
who can't afford a supplement (Medicare Savings Programs, Extra Help, people on both Medicare and
Medicaid); **G2** free, unbiased help and how brokers are paid. The main session re-checked their quotes
against the saved copies with 0 misses. Verdict counts are taken from the claim tables themselves (the E
and F headers had miscounted their rows)._

| # | claim | verdict | source | evidence file |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 449 | The federal Medigap open enrollment period: six months, starting with the first month a person is both 65 or older and enrolled in Part B (42 U.S.C. 1395ss(s)(2)(A)). | CONFIRMED. Quote: "during the 6 month period beginning with the first month as of the first day on which the individual is 65 years of age or older and is enrolled for benefits under part B" (hand pass E, row A1) | [42 U.S. Code § 1395ss (Medigap), Cornell LII](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/42/1395ss) | usc-42-1395ss-cornell.txt |
| 450 | What the window guarantees, in the statute: no denial, no conditions, no pricing by health status. | CONFIRMED. Quote: "may not deny or condition the issuance or effectiveness of a medicare supplemental policy, or discriminate in the pricing of the policy, because of health status" (hand pass E, row A2) | [42 U.S. Code § 1395ss (Medigap), Cornell LII](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/42/1395ss) | usc-42-1395ss-cornell.txt |

## Fairness audit
- **Neither side written beyond its evidence?** The pro-Advantage case is given at full strength in its
  own sources' words, including where the engine had understated it: Jung, Carlin and Feldman say quality
  is **better** in MA (log 85); the insurer-backed 36% is a stronger comparison than the engine thought,
  because it counts Medigap premiums (log 15); the cap is CMS-confirmed (log 34). The critics' case is
  held to the same standard: the 95% overturn rate is mostly one insurer reversing itself (log 98), the
  13% is a 2019 sample (log 82), the ghost-network figures are mental health only (log 183, 186), and
  "traditional Medicare has no prior authorization" is false (log 153).
- **Where the sources stand.** The record is lopsided in who says what, and the brief says so. Most
  pro-Advantage numbers come from the industry or from work it paid for: the 36% (ATI for Better Medicare
  Alliance), the selection rebuttals (AHIP, Humana's own subsidiary, Milliman for UnitedHealth, FTI,
  Paragon, HLC) (log 15–17, 413–428). Most of the critical numbers come from government referees (MedPAC,
  CBO, GAO, HHS's inspector general, a Senate majority staff) and from academics, some of them long-standing
  critics of MA payment (log 76). The peer-reviewed studies that favour MA are named as such (log 156,
  207, 215, 220), and the one most favourable on low-value care was written mostly by Humana's
  researchers (log 156). No false balance: on the taxpayer question the record leans one way, and the
  brief says so.
- **Straw-manning / cherry-picking?** Every cost figure carries its comparison group (Original Medicare
  with no supplement, with Medigap, or all FFS), because the answer flips with the comparator (log 19, 22,
  15). Allegations stay allegations: UnitedHealth's Justice Department matters are investigations and an
  undecided case, with no charges (log 435–447).
- **Identity↔stance rotation:** set at casting (step 4), not here.

## Open questions this episode could not settle
- Whether moving to MA saves an enrollee money against Original Medicare **with** Medigap: the best
  switcher study finds small differences with no consistent pattern (log 19, 80); nothing newer settles it.
- How much of MedPAC's gap is favorable selection: MedPAC 11% against Humana's 4.9–5.8% (log 329, 420);
  CBO lands near MedPAC overall but does not split selection from coding (log 402).
- Whether MA's lower use of care is efficiency or denial: both have peer-reviewed support (log 85, 87, 215,
  218); no study since the 2024 coverage-criteria rule repeats OIG's test (log 133, 134).
- How many people actually hit their plan's out-of-pocket cap (log 38).
- How much the extras are used: MedPAC says the data are inadequate, especially for OTC cards, meals and
  transport (log 70, 71).
- Realized dialysis access for MA enrollees with kidney failure (log 201).
- What the 2027 exits do in rural counties: no rural split exists (log 14).
- Whether the Fifth Circuit reinstates the 2023 audit-extrapolation rule (log 401), and how the court rules
  on the Poehling recommendation (log 446).
- Duals: the Commonwealth Fund's satisfaction edge is mostly an age effect and its figures are unverified
  (log 314, 315); an age-adjusted study now finds D-SNP duals report somewhat better access and
  satisfaction than duals in Original Medicare (log 506), but nothing in this record measures their
  spending or health outcomes.
- What a Medigap premium is as a share of a low income: no current named source (log 508).
- An average Part B giveback (log 487), and what CMS's 2026 broker-pay "concerns" were (log 529).
- ✅ **Answered by the top-ups (2026-10-21):** the federal six-month Medigap open enrollment period at 65
  (log 449–458); the 2027 Part B premium is still not announced, with the trustees' estimate of $209.50
  (log 459, 464); the Advantage cap covers Parts A and B only, Part D has its own $2,400 cap, together
  $12,250 in 2027 before premiums (log 474–478).

---

## Peg hunt 2026-10-21

_Kept as run. Where a hand pass later corrected or upgraded a line, the line carries a ⚠ note and the
correction is in the table above._

_Run by a single subagent, all five phases of the show's peg-hunt method, in one head. There was no
scout brief; the banned list was built from the question and the scoping note the episode was chosen
with (YouTube search interest in "medicare", 2026-10-20). The candidate pegs in that note were treated
as hypotheses to test, not answers. Re-run before scripting and on the day it posts._

### Phase 1a: the banned list
Topic words: Medicare, Medicare Advantage, Original Medicare, traditional Medicare, Part C, Part D, MA,
MA-PD, CMS, extras, supplemental benefits, premium(s), Medigap, open enrollment / enrolment period,
AEP, 2027 plans, plan exits, canceling/cancelled plans, star ratings, rate notice / rate announcement,
risk adjustment, coding, upcoding, chart review, prior authorization, networks.
Treated as allowed **domain** words (generic, not topic vocabulary): health insurance, health insurer(s),
health policy, seniors, retirees, people over 65, federal health program, private insurers. ⚠ "seniors"
and "insurers" sit very close to the topic; they were used as the skill's 1c domain words, and that is
the borderline call in this run, recorded so it can be audited. The search engine returned Medicare
pages for several of them anyway.

### Phase 1 queries and indexes (blind), with the exit test
| step | query or index | banned term in it? |
|---|---|---|
| 1d | Federal Register API, `PRESDOCU`, 2026-09-23 → 10-21 (18 documents, all read) | none |
| 1d | Federal Register API, `RULE`, `PRORULE`, `NOTICE`, same window (read by agency: HHS and its sub-agencies, DOJ) | none |
| 1d | KFF Health News "Morning Briefing" index, page 1 (Oct 16 → Oct 21) | none (an index, not a query) |
| 1d | CMS newsroom index page | none (an index, not a query; the agency's name is in the URL, the borderline call) |
| 1d | HHS press room index | none; returned 403, not read |
| 1b | "UnitedHealth Group news October 2026" | none |
| 1b | "Humana news October 2026" | none |
| 1b | "Aetna CVS Health October 2026 seniors" | none |
| 1b | "Elevance Health third quarter 2026 results October" | none |
| 1b | "Centene October 2026 news" | none |
| 1b | "Justice Department lawsuit health insurer October 2026" | none |
| 1b | "HHS inspector general report October 2026 insurers" | none |
| 1b | "Senate committee hearing health insurers retirees October 2026" | none |
| 1c | "seniors health insurance news week of October 20 2026" | none |
| 1c | "health policy October 2026 what changed for people over 65" | none |
| 1e | "\"first time\" federal health program private insurers 2026 unprecedented" | none |

**Exit test: passed.** No Phase 1 query contains a banned term. Counterparties named and queried by
name with a recency window: UnitedHealth Group, Humana, CVS Health/Aetna, Elevance Health, Centene, the
Justice Department, the HHS inspector general, the Senate committees.

**What the blind sweep found on its own:** the Federal Register index surfaced **2026-21420** (proposed
rule CMS-4214-P, published 2026-10-20), which implements the REAL Health Providers Act (section 6220 of
the Consolidated Appropriations Act, 2026): the provider-directory ("ghost network") rule. Both
ordinary indexes (CMS newsroom, KFF Health News) independently led with the same rule on 10-16/10-19.
The PRESDOCU sweep found **nothing** on health coverage (recorded so the gap is visible). The insurer
queries surfaced the Q3 earnings (UnitedHealth 10-13, Elevance 10-21), the 2027 star ratings, the 2027
open enrollment window (October 15 to December 7, 2026), and Humana's 2027 exits. A KFF Health News
sidebar tag "Medicare Advantage Billing Probe" was checked in Phase 2: nothing dated October 2026 was
found behind it.

"2027 Medicare Advantage landscape plan year premiums enrollment projection CMS September 2026" ·
"Medicare Advantage plans exiting 2027 members lose plan counties Humana UnitedHealthcare" ·

2026 2.48% risk adjustment unlinked chart review" · "2027 Medicare Advantage star ratings released
October 2026 share of enrollees in 4-star plans" · "KFF 2027 Medicare Advantage plans counties insurers
exiting analysis October 2026" · "medicare.gov Medigap guaranteed issue rights Medicare Advantage plan
leaving area 63 days" · opened: the CMS 2027 landscape release (09-28), the CMS-4214-P release (10-16),
the Federal Register API record for 2026-21420, the CMS 2027 rate announcement fact sheet (04-06),
KFF's 2027 county analysis (10-09), Insurance Business on Humana (08-31), eCFR 42 CFR 422.506.

### Phase 3: ranked candidates, with the rejects
| candidate | date | age | search intent | thesis fit | durability | call |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **The 2027 open enrollment window, with insurers pulling Advantage plans out of counties**: Oct 15 → Dec 7, 2026; KFF: 193 counties with no Advantage drug plan open to the general public (123 in 2026), HCSC out of 498 counties, Centene 359, UnitedHealth Group 228, Humana 62; Humana: about 600,000 members in exiting plans; CMS: average Advantage premium projected down 16.5% to $12.00 | window opened 2026-10-15; KFF 10-09; CMS 09-28 | 6 d | ★ highest: this is the window in which people choose between the two, and the autocomplete ("medicare advantage 2027", "medicare is quietly canceling plans") is this story | ★ high: the choice the episode is about is being made now, and the exits show the trade at its sharpest: the extras come from a private plan that can leave your county, while the way back to Original Medicare runs through Medigap underwriting | medium: annual, so it dates in six months; the description is re-peggable, and the script names the mechanism (a yearly switching window; plans can leave), not this year's numbers | **chosen, hot (description)** |
| **CMS-4214-P, the provider-directory ("ghost network") proposed rule**: verify directories every 90 days, public accuracy scores, in-network cost-sharing cap when a listing is wrong; from contract year 2028; comments to 2026-12-15 | announced 2026-10-16; Federal Register 2026-10-20 | 1–5 d | low: nobody searches a proposed rule | ★ high: the network is the core trade (Original Medicare takes any doctor who takes Medicare; an Advantage plan has a network whose listing may be wrong) | medium: a proposal; final rule later | **structural (script) + one line in the description**. Rejected as the lead: low search intent, not yet law, effective 2028 |

Release date and the 71% were secondary-verified only here; ⚠ since primary-verified at CMS's fact sheet by hand pass B (log 224) |
| 2027 rate announcement: +2.48% (over $13bn) to Advantage plans; unlinked chart-review diagnoses excluded from risk scores (−1.53% impact, with a switcher exception); 2024 risk model kept | 2026-04-06 | 198 d | low | ★ high for "who pays": the coding fight is the taxpayer half of the question | high | rejected as a peg (past the 45-day line); **structural (script)** and a fact for the engine |
| Insurer Q3 earnings: UnitedHealth (10-13, raised guidance), Elevance (10-21, raised guidance) | 10-13 / 10-21 | 0–8 d | low | low-medium: margins recovering; investor framing | low | rejected: off the viewer's question. Secondary only (search summaries of SEC 8-Ks) |
| Justice Department streamlined self-disclosure protocol for healthcare fraud (KFF Health News headline) | 2026-10-20 | 1 d | none | low: general fraud policy, not this choice | — | rejected: off-thesis (headline only, not opened) |

### Phase 4: verification status
- **Open enrollment October 15, 2026 → December 7, 2026; average Advantage premium $14.37 → $12.00
  (−16.5%); 5,553 → about 5,532 plans; 34 million projected 2027 enrollment (about 47.4%); supplemental
  benefits "expected to remain stable": primary-verified** (CMS press release, 2026-09-28, opened). The
  release does not mention plan exits. ⚠ **Hand pass C:** the 47.4% is of everyone on Medicare;
  MedPAC's and KFF's 55% is of Part A-and-B eligibles. Not a decline (log 304).
- **County exits (HCSC 498, Centene 359, UnitedHealth Group 228, Humana 62) and 193 counties /
  638,000 beneficiaries with no MA-PD open to general enrollment (123 counties / 399,000 in 2026):
  named-publisher verified** (KFF, 2026-10-09, opened). KFF built these from CMS landscape files; the
  files themselves were not opened. ⚠ **Hand pass C:** the 638,000 are everyone with Medicare living in
  those counties, not people losing a plan (log 2).
- **Humana about 600,000 members in 2027 exits, confirmed on its 2026-07-29 earnings call:
  secondary-verified** (Insurance Business, 2026-08-31, opened). Humana's own transcript or 8-K was not
  opened. ⚠ **Updated by hand pass C (2026-10-21):** the call transcript was read. The 600,000 are
  members whose specific **plan** ends, not people in the 62 counties Humana leaves; Humana expects to
  move a large share into its other plans ("just over 40%" in 2025); the figure is not in its 8-K or
  10-Q (log 9–12).
- **CMS-4214-P provisions and dates: primary-verified** (CMS release 2026-10-16 and the Federal
  Register API record for 2026-21420, opened; comments to 2026-12-15). The 90-day verification, the
  accuracy scores and the in-network cost-sharing cap come from the CMS release.
  ⚠ **Corrected by hand pass B:** plans report their scores to CMS from 2028; CMS posts them publicly
  from contract year **2029** (log 177).
- **Nonrenewal notice timing:** 42 CFR 422.506(a)(2)(ii) requires mailing "at least 90 calendar days
  before the date on which the nonrenewal is effective" (eCFR, opened). The "letters dated October 2"
  in trade press is not established as a regulatory date here and is not used.
- **Medigap guaranteed-issue right when an Advantage plan leaves (apply from 60 days before to 63 days
  after coverage ends): secondary-verified** (Indiana SHIP guide and other summaries; the medicare.gov
  page was not reached). ⚠ **Updated by hand pass C:** now primary-verified at Medicare.gov and in the
  statute. Medicare.gov says 60 days before to 63 days after; the statute starts an involuntary
  termination's window on the date the notice is received. For a plan ending Dec 31 the window closes
  **Mar 4, 2027** (our arithmetic). The separate window to pick another Advantage or drug plan runs Dec 8
  to the last day of February 2027 (log 254, 267, 268).
- **2027 rate announcement (2.48%, over $13bn, −1.53% sources-of-diagnoses impact, 2024 Part C model
  kept): primary-verified** (CMS fact sheet, 2026-04-06, opened).
